    "Title": "Accelerating Gossip SGD with Periodic Global Averaging",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the scalability limitations of Gossip SGD and Local SGD in large-scale distributed training, primarily caused by communication overhead and slow convergence on sparse networks. It introduces Gossip-PGA (Gossip SGD with Periodic Global Averaging), which integrates periodic All-Reduce global averaging into Gossip SGD, significantly improving its transient-stage convergence complexity (e.g., from O(n^3/(1-β)^4) to O(n^3) for non-convex problems on sparse networks). The paper also proposes Gossip-AGA, an adaptive extension that automatically adjusts the averaging period. Key findings include establishing comprehensive convergence rates for both smooth convex and non-convex problems, demonstrating how gossip and periodic averaging collaborate to improve transient stages, and empirically validating these theoretical findings. Experiments on Image Classification (ResNet50) and Language Modeling (BERT) show that Gossip-PGA/AGA achieves comparable accuracy to Parallel SGD with 1.3-1.9x runtime speed-up, while also remedying accuracy degradation issues observed in standard Gossip SGD and Local SGD.",
    "Methodology": "The core methodology involves Gossip-PGA, which combines localized Gossip communication with periodic global parameter averaging via All-Reduce. In each iteration, nodes perform local stochastic gradient descent. For 'H' iterations, they exchange model parameters only with direct neighbors (gossip step). Every 'H' iterations, a global average is performed using efficient All-Reduce primitives (e.g., Ring All-Reduce) to synchronize all model parameters across the network. Gossip-AGA extends this by adaptively adjusting the period 'H' based on training loss, aiming to perform more frequent global synchronizations in early training stages to reduce consensus variance. The theoretical analysis provides convergence rates for both smooth convex and non-convex objective functions, under standard assumptions including L-smoothness, bounded gradient noise, and a strongly connected network characterized by a doubly stochastic weight matrix.",
    "Experimental Setup": "The research employed two main experimental setups: convex logistic regression on synthetic data and large-scale deep learning tasks. For logistic regression, various network sizes (20, 50, 100 nodes), topologies (ring, grid, exponential graph), and data distributions (iid, non-iid) were used, with a fixed averaging period H=16 for Gossip-PGA. For deep learning, ResNet-50 was trained on ImageNet-1k for image classification (using 256 GPUs across 32 nodes, batch size 8192) and BERT-Large was trained on Wikipedia and BookCorpus for language modeling (using 64 GPUs across 8 nodes, batch size 4096). All experiments were implemented in PyTorch 1.5.1 with NCCL 2.5.7 and utilized mixed precision training with NVIDIA apex. Inter-node communication used 25 Gbps TCP or 4x100 Gbps RoCE networks. The proposed methods were compared against Parallel SGD, Gossip SGD, Local SGD, and OSGP. Evaluation metrics included Top-1 validation accuracy, wall-clock training time, training loss, and transient stage iterations.",
    "Limitations": "One limitation of Gossip-PGA is the need for manual tuning of the global averaging period 'H', although Gossip-AGA attempts to address this with an adaptive strategy. The effectiveness of periodic global averaging, particularly with All-Reduce, is predicated on high-performance data-center clusters with high-bandwidth channels. In scenarios with extremely expensive communication, such as wireless sensor networks, global averaging might be prohibitive, suggesting it may not be recommended or would require approximation. The paper also notes that while Gossip-PGA improves overall training time, it is less communication-efficient during the linear speedup stage compared to pure Gossip SGD due to the added overhead of periodic global averaging. Finally, the study does not integrate other orthogonal communication-efficient techniques like quantization, gradient compression, or lazy communication, although these are acknowledged as compatible extensions.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research directions include integrating Gossip-PGA/AGA with orthogonal communication-efficient techniques such as quantization, gradient compression, and lazy communication to further enhance its performance. Another direction is to refine and explore more sophisticated adaptive strategies for the global averaging period 'H' in Gossip-AGA, potentially improving its robustness and performance across a wider range of scenarios. Additionally, investigating alternative methods for approximating global averaging (e.g., via multiple gossip steps) or determining optimal strategies for environments where All-Reduce is prohibitively expensive (like wireless sensor networks) could be valuable. Further analysis into the interaction between periodic global averaging and momentum optimizers is also suggested, given observations that momentum updates are not always universally beneficial in related frameworks like SlowMo.",
    "Experiment Code": "class DistributedFusedAdam(torch.optim.Optimizer):\n\n    # Enable custom logic for AMP grad scaling\n    _step_supports_amp_scaling: bool = True\n    _custom_amp_unscale_grads: bool = True\n\n    def __init__(self, params: Union[Iterable[torch.nn.Parameter], Iterable[dict]], lr: float = 1e-3, bias_correction: bool = True, betas: Tuple[float, float] = (0.9, 0.999), eps: float = 1e-8, adam_w_mode: bool = True, weight_decay: float = 0.0, amsgrad: bool = False, dtype: torch.dtype = torch.float32, grad_sync_dtype: Optional[torch.dtype] = None, param_sync_dtype: Optional[torch.dtype] = None, device: Optional[torch.device] = \"cuda\", process_group: Optional[torch.distributed.ProcessGroup] = None, distributed_process_group: Optional[torch.distributed.ProcessGroup] = None, redundant_process_group: Optional[torch.distributed.ProcessGroup] = None, average_grad_sync: bool = True, overlap_grad_sync: bool = True, overlap_param_sync: bool = False, bucket_cap_mb: float = 100.0, pipeline_size: int = 2, contiguous_param_buffer: bool = False, contiguous_grad_buffer: bool = False, store_params: bool = True, store_param_remainders: bool = False, with_scaled_states: bool = False, nccl_ub: bool = False, capturable: bool = False):\n        # ... (constructor code for DistributedFusedAdam) ...\n\n    @torch.no_grad()\n    def _local_step(self, bucket_ids: List[int]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Apply optimizer step to local shard of parameter buckets\n\n        Arguments:\n            bucket_ids (list): bucket indices\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Implementation with scaled optimizer state\n        if self.with_scaled_states:\n            self._local_step_with_scaled_states(bucket_ids)\n            return\n\n        # Optimized implementation with BF16 params and 16-bit param\n        # remainders\n        if self.store_param_remainders:\n            bf16_rem_buckets = set()\n            for bucket_id in bucket_ids:\n                state_bucket = self.state[\"buckets\"][bucket_id]\n                if state_bucket.param_remainders_shard is not None:\n                    bf16_rem_buckets.add(bucket_id)\n            if bf16_rem_buckets:\n                self._local_step_with_param_remainders(sorted(bf16_rem_buckets))\n            bucket_ids = [\n                bucket_id\n                for bucket_id in bucket_ids\n                if bucket_id not in bf16_rem_buckets\n            ]\n            if not bucket_ids:\n                return\n\n        # Find param fragments for each bucket\n        buffers = collections.defaultdict(list)  # p_in, m, v, g, p_out\n        for bucket_id in bucket_ids:\n            state_bucket = self.state[\"buckets\"][bucket_id]\n            grads_bucket = self._grads_buckets[bucket_id]\n            params_bucket = self._params_buckets[bucket_id]\n\n            # Optimizer state buffers for local shard\n            fragments = state_bucket.fragments\n            exp_avg = state_bucket.exp_avg_shard\n            exp_avg_sq = state_bucket.exp_avg_sq_shard\n            grads = grads_bucket.grads_shard\n            params_out = params_bucket.params_shard\n\n            # Find param fragments in local shard\n            for fragment in fragments:\n                if not fragment.in_local_shard:\n                    continue\n                shard_start, shard_end = fragment.shard_range\n                if shard_end <= shard_start:\n                    continue\n                shard_range = slice(shard_start, shard_end)\n                if state_bucket.params_shard is None:\n                    param = self.parameter(fragment)\n                    if param.is_contiguous(memory_format=torch.channels_last):\n                        param = param.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)\n                    param_range = slice(*fragment.shard_param_range)\n                    param_fragment = param.detach().view(-1)[param_range]\n                    param_fragment = param_fragment.to(\n                        dtype=state_bucket.dtype, device=self.device\n                    )\n                else:\n                    params_shard = state_bucket.params_shard\n                    param_fragment = params_shard[shard_range]\n                buffers_key = (\n                    fragment.param_group_id,\n                    state_bucket.dtype,\n                    state_bucket.grad_sync_dtype,\n                    state_bucket.param_sync_dtype,\n                )\n                buffers[buffers_key].append(\n                    [\n                        param_fragment,\n                        exp_avg[shard_range],\n                        exp_avg_sq[shard_range],\n                        grads[shard_range],\n                        params_out[shard_range],\n                    ]\n                )\n\n        # Apply optimizer step to each param group\n        adam_func = distributed_adam_cuda.multi_tensor_fused_adam_capturable \\\n            if self.capturable else distributed_adam_cuda.multi_tensor_fused_adam\n        for (group_id, _, _, _), group_buffers in buffers.items():\n            group = self.param_groups[group_id]\n            beta1, beta2 = group[\"betas\"]\n            multi_tensor_applier(\n                adam_func,\n                self._dummy_overflow_buf,\n                list(zip(*group_buffers)),\n                self._grad_scale,\n                group[\"lr\"],\n                beta1,\n                beta2,\n                group[\"eps\"],\n                self.state[\"step\"],\n                1 if self.adam_w_mode else 0,\n                1 if group[\"bias_correction\"] else 0,\n                group[\"weight_decay\"],\n            )\n\n        # Make sure param sync buffer has correct dtype\n        self._check_params_shard_dtypes(\n            {\n                bucket_id: self._params_buckets[bucket_id]\n                for bucket_id in bucket_ids\n            }\n        )\n\n    def _start_bucket_grad_sync(self, buckets: List[GradientBucket]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Synchronize gradient buckets\n\n        Gradient synchronization is asynchronous. Involves\n        reduce-scatter over distributed process group and allreduce\n        over redundant process group. Assumes grad bucket buffers are\n        already initialized.\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Complete any outstanding grad syncs\n        # Note: Not needed with contiguous grad buffer since there is\n        # no memory benefit from eagerly freeing grad buffers.\n        if not self.contiguous_grad_buffer:\n            self._finish_bucket_grad_sync()\n\n        # Reduction operation\n        if self.average_grad_sync and not self.nccl_ub:\n            reduce_op = torch.distributed.ReduceOp.AVG\n        else:\n            reduce_op = torch.distributed.ReduceOp.SUM\n\n        # Initialize grad state and buffers\n        for bucket in buckets:\n            if bucket.status == self.GradientStatus.SYNCING:\n                self._finish_bucket_grad_sync()\n            bucket.status = self.GradientStatus.SYNCING\n            bucket.grads_generated.clear()\n            if self.distributed_size == 1:\n                bucket.sync_grads_shard = bucket.grads_bucket\n            elif bucket.sync_grads_shard is None:\n                bucket_size = bucket.grads_bucket.numel()\n                shard_size = bucket_size // self.distributed_size\n                bucket.sync_grads_shard = torch.empty(\n                    [shard_size],\n                    dtype=bucket.grads_bucket.dtype,\n                    device=bucket.grads_bucket.device,\n                )\n\n            # Handle case with multiple grad accumulation steps\n            if bucket.grads_shard is not None:\n                if bucket.sync_grads_shard.data_ptr() == bucket.grads_shard.data_ptr():\n                    bucket.grads_shard = bucket.grads_shard.clone()\n\n        # Side stream for communication\n        # If new bucket is ready before last bucket communication finishes, use multiple\n        # communication streams could help pipeline reduce-scatter and all-reduce.\n        main_stream = torch.cuda.current_stream()\n        self._last_comm_stream_id = (self._last_comm_stream_id + 1) % len(self._comm_streams)\n        comm_stream = self._comm_streams[self._last_comm_stream_id]\n        comm_stream.wait_stream(main_stream)\n\n        # Reduce-scatter over distributed process group\n        if buckets and self.distributed_size > 1:\n            with torch.cuda.stream(comm_stream):\n                group = self.distributed_process_group\n                with _coalescing_manager(group, self.device, async_ops=True) as cm:\n                    for bucket in buckets:\n                        if self.average_grad_sync and self.nccl_ub:\n                            bucket.grads_bucket /= self.distributed_size\n                        _coalescing_manager_append_work(\n                            cm,\n                            reduce_scatter_tensor(\n                                bucket.sync_grads_shard,\n                                bucket.grads_bucket,\n                                op=reduce_op,\n                                group=group,\n                                async_op=True,\n                            ),\n                        )\n                cm.wait()\n\n        # All-reduce over redundant process group\n        if buckets and self.redundant_size > 1:\n            with torch.cuda.stream(comm_stream):\n                group = self.redundant_process_group\n                with _coalescing_manager(group, self.device, async_ops=True) as cm:\n                    for bucket in buckets:\n                        _coalescing_manager_append_work(\n                            cm,\n                            torch.distributed.all_reduce(\n                                bucket.sync_grads_shard,\n                                op=reduce_op,\n                                group=group,\n                                async_op=True,\n                            ),\n                        )\n                cm.wait()\n\n    def _start_bucket_param_sync(self, buckets: List[ParameterBucket]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Synchronize parameter buckets\n\n        Parameter synchronization is asynchronous. Involves all-gather\n        over distributed process group. Assumes param shard buffers\n        are already initialized.\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Complete any outstanding param syncs\n        self._finish_bucket_param_sync()\n\n        # Initialize param state and buffers\n        buckets = [\n            bucket\n            for bucket in buckets\n            if bucket.status == self.ParameterStatus.SHARDED\n        ]\n        for bucket in buckets:\n            bucket.status = self.ParameterStatus.SYNCING\n            if bucket.params_bucket is not None:\n                pass\n            elif self.distributed_size == 1:\n                bucket.params_bucket = bucket.params_shard\n            else:\n                shard_size = bucket.params_shard.numel()\n                bucket_size = shard_size * self.distributed_size\n                bucket.params_bucket = torch.empty(\n                    [bucket_size],\n                    dtype=bucket.params_shard.dtype,\n                    device=bucket.params_shard.device,\n                )\n\n        # Side stream for communication\n        main_stream = torch.cuda.current_stream()\n        self._last_comm_stream_id = (self._last_comm_stream_id + 1) % len(self._comm_streams)\n        comm_stream = self._comm_streams[self._last_comm_stream_id]\n        comm_stream.wait_stream(main_stream)\n\n        #     "Experiment Result": "The implementation (`DistributedFusedAdam`) features local stochastic gradient descent (`_local_step`) combined with distributed global parameter averaging. This averaging is achieved through efficient collective communication primitives: `reduce_scatter_tensor` for gradient synchronization and `all_gather_into_tensor` for parameter synchronization.\n\n**Communication Settings:**\n- **Algorithm:** ZeRO-2 algorithm, distributing optimizer state and gradients.\n- **Groups:** Utilizes `process_group`, `distributed_process_group`, and `redundant_process_group` for flexible communication topologies.\n- **Reduction Operation:** Defaults to `ReduceOp.AVG` for gradient synchronization if `nccl_ub` is not used, otherwise `ReduceOp.SUM`.\n- **Overlap:** Supports overlapping gradient synchronization (`overlap_grad_sync=True`) and an experimental feature for overlapping parameter synchronization (`overlap_param_sync=False`) with computation.\n- **Pipelining:** Uses a `pipeline_size` (default: 2) to process multiple buckets simultaneously.\n- **Buffering:** Can use contiguous buffers for parameters (`contiguous_param_buffer=False`) and gradients (`contiguous_grad_buffer=False`).\n- **NCCL User Buffers:** Optionally enables NCCL user buffers (`nccl_ub=False`) for zero-copy communication (requires contiguous grad buffers and NCCL >= 2.19).\n\n**Optimizer State & Precision:**\n- **Optimizer:** Adam (or AdamW if `adam_w_mode=True`) algorithm.\n- **Precision:** Optimizer state `dtype` defaults to `torch.float32`. Configurable `grad_sync_dtype` and `param_sync_dtype` allow for mixed-precision communication.\n- **Scaling:** Supports `with_scaled_states` (default: `False`) for per-tensor scaling of optimizer state, particularly useful for low-precision state (fp16/bf16).\n- **Parameter Remainder Storage:** `store_param_remainders` (default: `False`) is an experimental feature for BF16 parameters with FP32 optimizer state.\n\n**Other Hyperparameters:** `lr`, `bias_correction`, `betas`, `eps`, `weight_decay`, and `capturable` (for CUDA Graphs, default: `False`).\n\n**Note on Discrepancies:** The method description mentions \"Gossip communication\" and \"adaptively adjusting the period 'H' based on training loss\".     "Title": "Bandit Samplers for Training Graph Neural Networks",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the challenge of accelerating Graph Neural Network (GNN) training, particularly the 'neighbor explosion' problem, by proposing novel variance-reduced samplers. Existing sampling algorithms for Graph Convolutional Networks (GCNs) are suboptimal and not applicable to more general GNNs like Graph Attention Networks (GAT) because the optimal sampling distribution involves intractable, constantly changing node embeddings and learned weights. The main contributions include: 1) Formulating the optimization of sampling variance as an adversary bandit problem. 2) Developing two bandit algorithms, GNN-BS (Multi-Armed Bandit) and GNN-BS.M (Multi-Armed Bandit with Multiple Plays), that maintain nonparametric estimates of the sampler and update it iteratively, making them applicable to both GCNs and attentive GNNs. 3) Theoretically demonstrating that their algorithms asymptotically approach the optimal variance within a factor of 3. 4) Empirically showing superior performance in convergence speed, attained accuracy (Micro F1 scores), and reduced sample variance compared to state-of-the-art approaches on multiple public datasets.",
    "Methodology": "The core methodology re-frames the GNN sampling problem as an adversary bandit problem. Instead of computing the intractable optimal sampling distribution, the proposed algorithms learn a sampler (policy) iteratively. Rewards are defined as the negative derivative of the effective sampling variance. Two main algorithms are introduced: 1) GNN-BS (Graph Neural Networks with Bandit Sampler): Operates under the adversary Multi-Armed Bandit (MAB) setting. It selects 1-element subsets of neighbors (arms) k times for each vertex, collects rewards (negative derivative of effective variance), and updates the alternative sampling distribution (q_t^i) using the EXP3 algorithm. 2) GNN-BS.M (Graph Neural Networks with Multiple Plays Bandit Sampler): Operates under the adversary MAB with multiple plays setting. It uses an efficient k-combination sampler (DepRound) to select a k-element subset of neighbors once. Rewards are derived from the negative derivative of an approximated effective variance (using Jensen's inequality), and the sampling distribution is updated using EXP3.M. For attentive GNNs, where attention values (α_ij) are learned and cannot be evaluated with only sampled neighborhoods, an adjusted feedback attention value (α'_ij) is defined to approximate the true attention values. The overall framework involves: using a bandit sampler to sample k neighbors, establishing an unbiased estimator, performing feedforward and backpropagation, calculating rewards, and updating the sampler with the bandit algorithms.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments were conducted on five benchmark datasets: Cora, Pubmed, PPI, Reddit, and Flickr, using standard data splits. An additional evaluation was performed on the OGB protein dataset. The GNN architectures used for comparison included GCN, GAT, and GeniePath (GP). For all comparison algorithms, the number of layers was fixed at 2. Hidden embedding dimensions were set to 16 for Cora and Pubmed, 256 for PPI, Reddit, and Flickr, and 64 for OGB protein. A normalization layer was not used to ensure a fair comparison. For attentive GNNs, the number of multi-heads was set to 1. Hyperparameters like learning rate ({0.01, 0.001}), L2-norm regularizers ({0, 0.0001, 0.0005, 0.001}), and dropout rate ({0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3}) were optimized via grid search. Sample sizes (k) for GraphSAGE, S-GCN, and the proposed algorithms were 1 for Cora/Pubmed, 5 for Flickr, and 10 for PPI/Reddit/OGB protein. Specific sample sizes for FastGCN, AS-GCN, and AS-GAT were 256/256 for Cora/Pubmed, 1,900/3,800 for PPI, 780/1,560 for Flickr, and 2,350/4,700 for Reddit. Batch size for layer sampling methods and S-GCN was 256. ClusterGCN partitions were configured based on suggestions for PPI/Reddit, and through grid search for Cora (10), Pubmed (10), and Flickr (200). GraphSAINT used an '0-1-1' architecture with the 'rw' sampling strategy. Micro F1 score was the primary evaluation metric. All experiments were run 3 times (10 times for OGB protein) to report mean and standard deviation. Model selection was based on validation set performance, and testing results were reported using the best validation model. Convergence was analyzed in terms of epochs and training time, and a sample size analysis was conducted on the PPI dataset to evaluate F1 scores and sample variances.",
    "Limitations": "The derivation of the proposed bandit samplers primarily follows the setting of node-wise sampling approaches, with the extension to layer-wise sampling left for future work. Strictly speaking, the GNN-BS algorithm (based on the standard Multi-Armed Bandit) is not entirely rigorous in a pure bandit setting because it repeats action (neighbor selection) k times before updating the policy, whereas the GNN-BS.M (Multi-Armed Bandit with multiple plays) is considered more rigorous. The effective variance for GNN-BS.M relies on an approximation derived using Jensen's inequality. While theoretically multiple qi's should be maintained for each layer, in practice, the paper notes that maintaining and updating a single qi using rewards only from the 1st layer works 'well enough,' which might be a practical compromise affecting optimality across layers. The theoretical regret analysis for the algorithms holds under a condition requiring a minimum number of training steps (T ≥ln(n/k)n^2(1 −η)/(kη^2)), implying asymptotic performance rather than immediate optimality, and the regret still accumulates with T (within a factor of 3).",
    "Future Research Directions": "The paper suggests two main directions for future research: 1) Extending the proposed bandit samplers from node-wise sampling to layer-wise sampling approaches. 2) Exploring other bandit settings beyond the current focus on the adversary bandit setting to potentially further optimize the sampling process.",
    "Experiment Code": "from cython_sampler import BanditMPSampler\n\ndef attention_mechanism(name, v, W_s, W_d, V, cur_embed, left, right, n2n):\n    if name == 'generalized_linear':\n        tl =
