sample_degree[src] = 0\n    for (src, dst) in edges:\n        sample_degree[src] += 1\n\n    for (src, dst) in edges:\n        n2n_indices_batch.append([node_map[src], node_map[dst]])\n        src_list.append(src)\n        dst_list.append(dst)\n        n2n_values_batch.append(1.)\n    n2n_indices_batch = np.array(n2n_indices_batch)\n    n2n_values_batch = np.array(n2n_values_batch)\n\n    left_indices_batch = [None]*len(n2n_indices_batch)\n    left_values_batch = np.ones(len(n2n_indices_batch))\n    right_indices_batch = [None]*len(n2n_indices_batch)\n    right_values_batch = np.ones(len(n2n_indices_batch))\n    ii = 0\n    for n1, n2 in n2n_indices_batch:\n        left_indices_batch[ii] = [ii, n1]\n        right_indices_batch[ii] = [ii, n2]\n        ii += 1\n\n    node_indices_batch = []\n    node_values_batch = np.ones(len(selected_nodes))\n    ii = 0\n    for nod in selected_nodes:\n        node_indices_batch.append([ii, node_map[nod]])\n        ii += 1\n    node_indices_batch = np.array(node_indices_batch)\n    node_values_batch = np.array(node_values_batch)\n\n    n2n = tf.SparseTensorValue(n2n_indices_batch, n2n_values_batch, [m_id, m_id])\n    left = tf.SparseTensorValue(left_indices_batch, left_values_batch, [len(left_indices_batch), m_id])\n    right = tf.SparseTensorValue(right_indices_batch, right_values_batch, [len(right_indices_batch), m_id])\n    node_select = tf.SparseTensorValue(node_indices_batch, node_values_batch, [len(node_indices_batch), m_id])\n    return expand_list, n2n, left, right, node_select, src_list, dst_list, node_map\n\n# In main training loop:\n# sampler initialization\nsampler = BanditMPSampler()\nsampler.init(adj_train)\n\n# subgraph generation using sampler\n# batch_nodes, y_batch = batch\n# subgraph_nodes, support, left, right, node_select, src_list, dst_list, node_map = \\\n#    gen_subgraph(sampler, batch_nodes, adj_train, neighbor_limit=FLAGS.neighbor_limit)\n\n# Training step (outs[2] contains model.sparse_attention_l0)\n# outs = sess.run([model.opt_op, model.loss, model.sparse_attention_l0, model.outputs], feed_dict=feed_dict)\n\n# Sampler update using attention values\nsampler.update(np.array(src_list, dtype=np.int32), np.array(dst_list, dtype=np.int32), outs[2])\n",
    "Experiment Result": "Dataset: 'cora' (default), 'ogbn-proteins', or 'ogbn-products'.\nLearning Rate: 0.001.\nEpochs: 3.\nHidden Layer 1 Units: 64.\nDropout Rate: 0.0.\nWeight Decay (L2 loss): 5e-4.\nEarly Stopping Tolerance: 30 epochs.\nNeighbor Limit (k): 10 (maximum number of neighbors to sample).\nBatch Size: 256.\nResidual Connections: Enabled (value 1).\nEta (Bandit Parameter): 0.4.\nDelta (Bandit Parameter): 0.01.\nMax Reward (Bandit Parameter): 1.0.\nNumber of Processes (for sampling): 12.\nModel Architecture: GeniePath (2 GNN layers with an attention mechanism and an LSTM-like update).\nOptimizer: TensorFlow AdamOptimizer.\nGradient Clipping: Gradients are clipped by value to be between -5.0 and 5.0.\nEvaluation Metric: 'rocauc' for 'ogbn-proteins', 'acc' for 'ogbn-products', using OGB Evaluator.\nTraining Runs: 10 independent runs, with model parameters reset before each run.\nSampling Mechanism: BanditMPSampler (implemented in Cython), iteratively updated with attention values from the GNN."
}{
    "Title": "GraphSAINT: Graph Sampling Based Inductive Learning Method",
    "Main Contributions": "GraphSAINT (Graph Sampling based Inductive Learning Method) addresses the challenge of scaling Graph Convolutional Networks (GCNs) to large graphs and deeper layers by resolving the \"neighbor explosion\" problem. It proposes a fundamentally different minibatch construction by sampling the training graph first to create subgraphs, then building a complete GCN on these subgraphs. This ensures a fixed number of well-connected nodes in all layers. Key contributions include novel normalization techniques to eliminate bias introduced by graph sampling and lightweight sampling algorithms for variance reduction. GraphSAINT decouples sampling from forward/backward propagation, allowing easy integration with various GCN architectures (e.g., graph attention, jumping connection). The method demonstrates superior accuracy and training time on five large graphs, achieving new state-of-the-art F1 scores for PPI (0.995) and Reddit (0.970).",
    "Methodology": "GraphSAINT constructs minibatches by first sampling a subgraph (Gs) from the training graph (G), then building a full GCN on Gs. This contrasts with layer sampling methods that sample nodes/edges within a GCN constructed on the full graph. To address bias from non-uniform node sampling probability, the method introduces two normalization techniques: aggregator normalization (αu,v = pu,v / pv) for feature propagation and loss normalization (λv = |V| * pv) for minibatch loss calculation, where pu,v and pv are estimated via pre-processing. For variance reduction, optimal edge sampling probabilities are derived (pe ∝ 1/deg(u) + 1/deg(v)) based on graph topology. Four light-weight samplers are integrated: Random Node Sampler, Random Edge Sampler, Random Walk Sampler, and Multi-dimensional Random Walk Sampler, all of which return the subgraph induced from the sampled components to preserve connectivity. GraphSAINT is designed to be compatible with advanced GCN architectures like Jumping Knowledge (JK-net) and Graph Attention Networks (GAT).",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments are conducted under an inductive, supervised learning setting for node classification tasks. Five large graph datasets are used: PPI (protein-protein interaction), Flickr (image categorization), Reddit (online post communities), Yelp (business categorization), and Amazon (product categories), all with fixed train/validation/test splits. Performance is measured using F1-micro score. GraphSAINT is compared against six baselines: Vanilla GCN, GraphSAGE, FastGCN, S-GCN, AS-GCN, and ClusterGCN, using their officially released code. The hardware setup includes a single NVIDIA Tesla P100 GPU, Dual Intel Xeon CPUs, and 512GB DDR4 memory. The software environment is Python 3.6.8 with Cython 0.29.2, TensorFlow 1.12.0, CUDA 9.2, and CUDNN 7.2.1. The sampler runs in parallel on 40 CPU cores. Hyperparameters like hidden dimension, dropout, and learning rate are tuned via grid search. Initial comparisons are primarily on 2-layer GCNs, with deeper models (4-layer, 5-layer) also tested for architectural variants.",
    "Limitations": "The definition of \"influence\" for sampling is simplified to focus on graph connectivity rather than incorporating node attributes due to the high complexity of the latter. While variance reduction for general feature aggregation is derived, a practical simplification is made by ignoring feature values and relying only on graph topology for edge probabilities to reduce sampling complexity. Explicit variance reduction for Graph Attention Networks (GATs) is challenging due to dynamically updated attention values, though normalization techniques are still applicable. The Multi-dimensional Random Walk Sampler (MRW) is empirically observed to be more computationally expensive than other samplers, incurring higher overhead.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work could involve developing distributed training algorithms for GraphSAINT. This includes partitioning the training graph across distributed memory, allowing independent sampling on each processor, and training on self-supportive subgraphs to significantly reduce system-level communication costs. This would also require developing data shuffling strategies for graph nodes and edges in conjunction with specific graph samplers. Another direction is to perform algorithm-system co-optimization to accelerate GraphSAINT's training on heterogeneous computing platforms (e.g., CPU-FPGA). Resolving \"neighbor explosion\" in GraphSAINT is expected to improve hardware utilization by reducing data traffic to slow memory and facilitate task-level parallelization due to the decoupling of sampling from GCN propagation.",
    "Experiment Code": "from graphsaint.globals import *\nimport numpy as np\nimport scipy.sparse\nimport time\nimport math\nimport pdb\nfrom math import ceil\nimport graphsaint.cython_sampler as cy\n\n\nclass GraphSampler:\n    \"\"\"\n    This is the sampler super-class. Any GraphSAINT sampler is supposed to perform\n    the following meta-steps:\n     1. [optional] Preprocessing: e.g., for edge sampler, we need to calculate the\n            sampling probability for each edge in the training graph. This is to be\n            performed only once per phase (or, once throughout the whole training,\n            since in most cases, training only consists of a single phase. see\n            ../train_config/README.md for definition of a phase).\n            ==> Need to override the `preproc()` in sub-class\n     2. Parallel sampling: launch a batch of graph samplers in parallel and sample\n            subgraphs independently. For efficiency, the actual sampling operation\n            happen in cython. And the classes here is mainly just a wrapper.\n            ==> Need to set self.cy_sampler to the appropriate cython sampler\n              in `__init__()` of the sampler sub-class\n     3. Post-processing: upon getting the sampled subgraphs, we need to prepare the\n            appropriate information (e.g., subgraph adj with renamed indices) to\n            enable the PyTorch trainer. Also, we need to do data conversion from C++\n            to Python (or, mostly numpy). Post-processing is handled within the\n            cython sampling file (./cython_sampler.pyx)\n\n    Pseudo-code for the four proposed sampling algorithms (Node, Edge, RandomWalk,\n    MultiDimRandomWalk) can be found in Appendix, Algo 2 of the GraphSAINT paper.\n\n    Lastly, if you don't bother with writing samplers in cython, you can still code\n    the sampler subclass in pure python. In this case, we have provided a function\n    `_helper_extract_subgraph` for API consistency between python and cython. An\n    example sampler in pure python is provided as `NodeSamplingVanillaPython` at the\n    bottom of this file.\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, args_preproc):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            adj_train       scipy sparse CSR matrix of the training graph\n            node_train      1D np array storing the indices of the training nodes\n            size_subgraph   int, the (estimated) number of nodes in the subgraph\n            args_preproc    dict, addition arguments needed for pre-processing\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.adj_train = adj_train\n        self.node_train = np.unique(node_train).astype(np.int32)\n        # size in terms of number of vertices in subgraph\n        self.size_subgraph = size_subgraph\n        self.name_sampler = 'None'\n        self.node_subgraph = None\n        self.preproc(**args_preproc)\n\n    def preproc(self, **kwargs):\n        pass\n\n    def par_sample(self, stage, **kwargs):\n        return self.cy_sampler.par_sample()\n\n    def _helper_extract_subgraph(self, node_ids):\n        \"\"\"\n        ONLY used for serial Python sampler (NOT for the parallel cython sampler).\n        Return adj of node-induced subgraph and other corresponding data struct.\n\n        Inputs:\n            node_ids        1D np array, each element is the ID in the original\n                            training graph.\n        Outputs:\n            indptr          np array, indptr of the subg adj CSR\n            indices         np array, indices of the subg adj CSR\n            data            np array, data of the subg adj CSR. Since we have aggregator\n                            normalization, we can simply set all data values to be 1\n            subg_nodes      np array, i-th element stores the node ID of the original graph\n                            for the i-th node in the subgraph. Used to index the full feats\n                            and label matrices.\n            subg_edge_index np array, i-th element stores the edge ID of the original graph\n                            for the i-th edge in the subgraph. Used to index the full array\n                            of aggregation normalization.\n        \"\"\"\n        node_ids = np.unique(node_ids)\n        node_ids.sort()\n        orig2subg = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(node_ids)}\n        n = node_ids.size\n        indptr = np.zeros(node_ids.size + 1)\n        indices = []\n        subg_edge_index = []\n        subg_nodes = node_ids\n        for nid in node_ids:\n            idx_s, idx_e = self.adj_train.indptr[nid], self.adj_train.indptr[nid + 1]\n            neighs = self.adj_train.indices[idx_s : idx_e]\n            for i_n, n in enumerate(neighs):\n                if n in orig2subg:\n                    indices.append(orig2subg[n])\n                    indptr[orig2subg[nid] + 1] += 1\n                    subg_edge_index.append(idx_s + i_n)\n        indptr = indptr.cumsum().astype(np.int64)\n        indices = np.array(indices)\n        subg_edge_index = np.array(subg_edge_index)\n        data = np.ones(indices.size)\n        assert indptr[-1] == indices.size == subg_edge_index.size\n        return indptr, indices, data, subg_nodes, subg_edge_index\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------\n# [BELOW] python wrapper for parallel samplers implemented with Cython\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nclass rw_sampling(GraphSampler):\n    \"\"\"\n    The sampler performs unbiased random walk, by following the steps:\n     1. Randomly pick `size_root` number of root nodes from all training nodes;\n     2. Perform length `size_depth` random walk from the roots. The current node\n            expands the next hop by selecting one of the neighbors uniformly\n            at random;\n     3. Generate node-induced subgraph from the nodes touched by the random walk.\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, size_root, size_depth):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            adj_train       see super-class\n            node_train      see super-class\n            size_subgraph   see super-class\n            size_root       int, number of root nodes (i.e., number of walkers)\n            size_depth      int, number of hops to take by each walker\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.size_root = size_root\n        self.size_depth = size_depth\n        size_subgraph = size_root * size_depth\n        super().__init__(adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, {})\n        self.cy_sampler = cy.RW(\n            self.adj_train.indptr,\n            self.adj_train.indices,\n            self.node_train,\n            NUM_PAR_SAMPLER,\n            SAMPLES_PER_PROC,\n            self.size_root,\n            self.size_depth\n        )\n\n    def preproc(self, **kwargs):\n        pass\n\n\nclass edge_sampling(GraphSampler):\n    def __init__(self,adj_train,node_train,num_edges_subgraph):\n        \"\"\"\n        The sampler picks edges from the training graph independently, following\n        a pre-computed edge probability distribution. i.e.,\n            p_{u,v} \\propto 1 / deg_u + 1 / deg_v\n        Such prob. dist. is derived to minimize the variance of the minibatch\n        estimator (see Thm 3.2 of the GraphSAINT paper).\n        \"\"\"\n        self.num_edges_subgraph = num_edges_subgraph\n        # num subgraph nodes may not be num_edges_subgraph * 2 in many cases,\n        # but it is not too important to have an accurate estimation of subgraph\n        # size. So it's probably just fine to use this number.\n        self.size_subgraph = num_edges_subgraph * 2\n        self.deg_train = np.array(adj_train.sum(1)).flatten()\n        self.adj_train_norm = scipy.sparse.dia_matrix((1 / self.deg_train, 0), shape=adj_train.shape).dot(adj_train)\n        super().__init__(adj_train, node_train, self.size_subgraph, {})\n        self.cy_sampler = cy.Edge2(\n            self.adj_train.indptr,\n            self.adj_train.indices,\n            self.node_train,\n            NUM_PAR_SAMPLER,\n            SAMPLES_PER_PROC,\n            self.edge_prob_tri.row,\n            self.edge_prob_tri.col,\n            self.edge_prob_tri.data.cumsum(),\n            self.num_edges_subgraph,\n        )\n\n    def preproc(self,**kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the edge probability distribution p_{u,v}.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.edge_prob = scipy.sparse.csr_matrix(\n            (\n                np.zeros(self.adj_train.size),\n                self.adj_train.indices,\n                self.adj_train.indptr\n            ),\n            shape=self.adj_train.shape,\n        )\n        self.edge_prob.data[:] = self.adj_train_norm.data[:]\n        _adj_trans = scipy.sparse.csr_matrix.tocsc(self.adj_train_norm)\n        self.edge_prob.data += _adj_trans.data      # P_e \\propto a_{u,v} + a_{v,u}\n        self.edge_prob.data *= 2 * self.num_edges_subgraph / self.edge_prob.data.sum()\n        # now edge_prob is a symmetric matrix, we only keep the\n        # upper triangle part, since adj is assumed to be undirected.\n        self.edge_prob_tri = scipy.sparse.triu(self.edge_prob).astype(np.float32)  # NOTE: in coo format\n\n\nclass mrw_sampling(GraphSampler):\n    \"\"\"\n    A variant of the random walk sampler. The multi-dimensional random walk sampler\n    is proposed in https://www.cs.purdue.edu/homes/ribeirob/pdf/ribeiro_imc2010.pdf\n\n    Fast implementation of the sampler is proposed in https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.11899\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, size_frontier, max_deg=10000):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            adj_train       see super-class\n            node_train      see super-class\n            size_subgraph   see super-class\n            size_frontier   int, size of the frontier during sampling process. The\n                            size of the frontier is fixed during sampling.\n            max_deg         int, the sampler picks iteratively pick a node from the\n                            frontier by probability proportional to node degree. If\n                            we specify the `max_deg`, we are essentially bounding the\n                            probability of picking any frontier node. This may help\n                            with improving sampling quality for skewed graphs.\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.p_dist = None\n        super().__init__(adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, {})\n        self.size_frontier = size_frontier\n        self.deg_train = np.bincount(self.adj_train.nonzero()[0])\n        self.name_sampler = 'MRW'\n        self.max_deg = int(max_deg)\n        self.cy_sampler = cy.MRW(\n            self.adj_train.indptr,\n            self.adj_train.indices,\n            self.node_train,\n            NUM_PAR_SAMPLER,\n            SAMPLES_PER_PROC,\n            self.p_dist,\n            self.max_deg,\n            self.size_frontier,\n            self.size_subgraph\n        )\n\n    def preproc(self,**kwargs):\n        _adj_hop = self.adj_train\n        self.p_dist = np.array(\n            [\n                _adj_hop.data[_adj_hop.indptr[v] : _adj_hop.indptr[v + 1]].sum()\n                for v in range(_adj_hop.shape[0])\n            ],\n            dtype=np.int32,\n        )\n\n\nclass node_sampling(GraphSampler):\n    \"\"\"\n    Independently pick some nodes from the full training graph, based on\n    pre-computed node probability distribution. The prob. dist. follows\n    Sec 3.4 of the GraphSAINT paper. For detailed derivation, see FastGCN\n    (https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.10247).\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            adj_train       see super-class\n            node_train      see super-class\n            size_subgraph   see super-class\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.p_dist = np.zeros(len(node_train))\n        super().__init__(adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, {})\n        self.cy_sampler = cy.Node(\n            self.adj_train.indptr,\n            self.adj_train.indices,\n            self.node_train,\n            NUM_PAR_SAMPLER,\n            SAMPLES_PER_PROC,\n            self.p_dist,\n            self.size_subgraph,\n        )\n\n    def preproc(self, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Node probability distribution is derived in https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.10247\n        \"\"\"\n        _p_dist = np.array(\n            [\n                self.adj_train.data[\n                    self.adj_train.indptr[v] : self.adj_train.indptr[v + 1]\n                ].sum()\n                for v in self.node_train\n            ],\n            dtype=np.int64,\n        )\n        self.p_dist = _p_dist.cumsum()\n        if self.p_dist[-1] > 2**31 - 1:\n            print('warning: total deg exceeds 2**31')\n            self.p_dist = self.p_dist.astype(np.float64)\n            self.p_dist /= self.p_dist[-1] / (2**31 - 1)\n        self.p_dist = self.p_dist.astype(np.int32)\n\n\nclass full_batch_sampling(GraphSampler):\n    \"\"\"\n    Strictly speaking, this is not a sampler. It simply returns the full adj\n    matrix of the training graph. This can serve as a baseline to compare\n    full-batch vs. minibatch performance.\n\n    Therefore, the size_subgraph argument is not used here.\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph):\n        super().__init__(adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, {})\n        self.cy_sampler = cy.FullBatch(\n            self.adj_train.indptr,\n            self.adj_train.indices,\n            self.node_train,\n            NUM_PAR_SAMPLER,\n            SAMPLES_PER_PROC,\n        )\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------\n# [BELOW] Example sampler based on pure python\n# --------------------------------------------\n\nclass NodeSamplingVanillaPython(GraphSampler):\n    \"\"\"\n    This class is just to showcase how you can write the graph sampler in pure python.\n\n    The simplest and most basic sampler: just pick nodes uniformly at random and return the\n    node-induced subgraph.\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph):\n        super().__init__(adj_train, node_train, size_subgraph, {})\n\n    def par_sample(self, stage, **kwargs):\n        node_ids = np.random.choice(self.node_train, self.size_subgraph)\n        ret = self._helper_extract_subgraph(node_ids)\n        ret = list(ret)\n        for i in range(len(ret)):\n            ret[i] = [ret[i]]\n        return ret\n\n    def preproc(self):\n        pass\nimport torch\nfrom torch import nn\nimport scipy.sparse as sp\n\n\nF_ACT = {'relu': nn.ReLU(),\n         'I': lambda x:x}\n\n\"\"\"\nNOTE\n    For the various GNN layers, we optionally support batch normalization. Yet, due to the\n    non-IID nature of GNN samplers (whether it is graph-sampling or layer sampling based),\n    we may need some modification to the standard batch-norm layer operations to achieve\n    optimal accuracy on graphs.\n\n    The study of optimal GNN-based batch-norm is out-of-scope for the current version of\n    GraphSAINT. So as a compromise, we provide multiple implementations of batch-norm\n    layer which can be optionally inserted at the output of the GNN layers.\n\nSpecifically, we have the various choices for the <bias> field in the layer classes\n    'bias'          means no batch-norm is applied. Only add the bias to the hidden\n                    features of the GNN layer.\n    'norm'          means we calculate the mean and variance of the GNN hidden features,\n                    and then scale the hidden features manually by the mean and variance.\n                    In this case, we need explicitly create the 'offset' and 'scale' params.\n    'norm-nn'       means we use the torch.nn.BatchNorm1d layer implemented by torch.\n                    In this case, no need to explicitly maintain the BN internal params.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass HighOrderAggregator(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, dim_in, dim_out, dropout=0., act='relu', \\\n            order=1, aggr='mean', bias='norm-nn', **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Layer implemented here combines the GraphSAGE-mean [1] layer with MixHop [2] layer.\n        We define the concept of `order`: an order-k layer aggregates neighbor information\n        from 0-hop all the way to k-hop. The operation is approximately:\n            X W_0 [+] A X W_1 [+] ... [+] A^k X W_k\n        where [+] is some aggregation operation such as addition or concatenation.\n\n        Special cases:\n            Order = 0  -->  standard MLP layer\n            Order = 1  -->  standard GraphSAGE layer\n\n        [1]: https://cs.stanford.edu/people/jure/pubs/graphsage-nips17.pdf\n        [2]: https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.00067\n\n        Inputs:\n            dim_in      int, feature dimension for input nodes\n            dim_out     int, feature dimension for output nodes\n            dropout     float, dropout on weight matrices W_0 to W_k\n            act         str, activation function. See F_ACT at the top of this file\n            order       int, see definition above\n            aggr        str, if 'mean' then [+] operation adds features of various hops\n                            if 'concat' then [+] concatenates features of various hops\n            bias        str, if 'bias' then apply a bias vector to features of each hop\n                            if 'norm' then perform batch-normalization on output features\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        super(HighOrderAggregator,self).__init__()\n        assert bias in ['bias', 'norm', 'norm-nn']\n        self.order, self.aggr = order, aggr\n        self.act, self.bias = F_ACT[act], bias\n        self.dropout = dropout\n        self.f_lin, self.f_bias = [], []\n        self.offset, self.scale = [], []\n        self.num_param = 0\n        for o in range(self.order + 1):\n            self.f_lin.append(nn.Linear(dim_in, dim_out, bias=False))\n            nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self.f_lin[-1].weight)\n            self.f_bias.append(nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(dim_out)))\n            self.num_param += dim_in * dim_out\n            self.num_param += dim_out\n            self.offset.append(nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(dim_out)))\n            self.scale.append(nn.Parameter(torch.ones(dim_out)))\n            if self.bias == 'norm' or self.bias == 'norm-nn':\n                self.num_param += 2 * dim_out\n        self.f_lin = nn.ModuleList(self.f_lin)\n        self.f_dropout = nn.Dropout(p=self.dropout)\n        self.params = nn.ParameterList(self.f_bias + self.offset + self.scale)\n        self.f_bias = self.params[:self.order + 1]\n        if self.bias == 'norm':\n            self.offset = self.params[self.order + 1 : 2 * self.order + 2]\n            self.scale = self.params[2 * self.order + 2 : ]\n        elif self.bias == 'norm-nn':\n            final_dim_out = dim_out * ((aggr=='concat') * (order + 1) + (aggr=='mean'))\n            self.f_norm = nn.BatchNorm1d(final_dim_out, eps=1e-9, track_running_stats=True)\n        self.num_param = int(self.num_param)\n\n    def _spmm(self, adj_norm, _feat):\n        \"\"\" sparce feature matrix multiply dense feature matrix \"\"\"\n        # alternative ways: use geometric.propagate or torch.mm\n        return torch.sparse.mm(adj_norm, _feat)\n\n    def _f_feat_trans(self, _feat, _id):\n        feat = self.act(self.f_lin[_id](_feat) + self.f_bias[_id])\n        if self.bias == 'norm':\n            mean = feat.mean(dim=1).view(feat.shape[0],1)\n            var = feat.var(dim=1, unbiased=False).view(feat.shape[0], 1) + 1e-9\n            feat_out = (feat - mean) * self.scale[_id] * torch.rsqrt(var) + self.offset[_id]\n        else:\n            feat_out = feat\n        return feat_out\n\n    def forward(self, inputs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:.\n            adj_norm        normalized adj matrix of the subgraph\n            feat_in         2D matrix of input node features\n\n        Outputs:\n            adj_norm        same as input (to facilitate nn.Sequential)\n            feat_out        2D matrix of output node features\n        \"\"\"\n        adj_norm, feat_in = inputs\n        feat_in = self.f_dropout(feat_in)\n        feat_hop = [feat_in]\n        # generate A^i X\n        for o in range(self.order):\n            # propagate(edge_index, x=x, norm=norm)\n            feat_hop.append(self._spmm(adj_norm, feat_hop[-1]))\n        feat_partial = [self._f_feat_trans(ft, idf) for idf, ft in enumerate(feat_hop)]\n        if self.aggr == 'mean':\n            feat_out = feat_partial[0]\n            for o in range(len(feat_partial) - 1):\n                feat_out += feat_partial[o + 1]\n        elif self.aggr == 'concat':\n            feat_out = torch.cat(feat_partial, 1)\n        else:\n            raise NotImplementedError\n        if self.bias == 'norm-nn':\n            feat_out = self.f_norm(feat_out)\n        return adj_norm, feat_out       # return adj_norm to support Sequential\n\n\nclass JumpingKnowledge(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        To be added soon. For now please see the tensorflow version for JK layers\n        \"\"\"\n        pass\n\n\nclass AttentionAggregator(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"\n    This layer follows the design of Graph Attention Network (GAT: https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.10903).\n    We extend GAT to higher order as well (see the HighOrderAggregator class above), even though most\n    of the time, order-1 layer should be sufficient. The enhancement to SAGE-mean architecture is\n    that GAT performs *weighted* aggregation on neighbor features. The edge weight is generated by\n    additional learnable MLP layer. Such weight means \"attention\". GAT proposed multi-head attention\n    so that there can be multiple weights for each edge. The k-head attention can be speficied by the\n    `mulhead` parameter.\n\n    Note that\n     1. In GraphSAINT minibatch training, we remove the softmax normalization across the neighbors.\n        Reason: since the minibatch does not see the full neighborhood, softmax does not make much\n        sense now. We see significant accuracy improvement by removing the softmax step. See also\n        Equations 8 and 9, Appendix C.3 of GraphSAINT (https://arxiv.org/pdf/1907.04931.pdf).\n     2. For order > 1, we obtain attention from neighbors from lower order up to higher order.\n\n     Inputs:\n        dim_in      int, feature dimension for input nodes\n        dim_out     int, feature dimension for output nodes\n        dropout     float, dropout on weight matrices W_0 to W_k\n        act         str, activation function. See F_ACT at the top of this file\n        order       int, see definition in HighOrderAggregator\n        aggr        str, if 'mean' then [+] operation adds features of various hops\n                         if 'concat' then [+] concatenates features of various hops\n        bias        str, if 'bias' then apply a bias vector to features of each hop\n                         if 'norm' then perform batch-normalization on output features\n        mulhead     int, the number of heads for attention\n\n    Outputs:\n        None\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, dim_in, dim_out, dropout=0., act='relu', \\\n            order=1, aggr='mean', bias='norm', mulhead=1):\n        super(AttentionAggregator,self).__init__()\n        assert bias in ['bias', 'norm', 'norm-nn']\n        self.num_param = 0\n        self.mulhead = mulhead\n        self.order, self.aggr = order, aggr\n        self.act, self.bias = F_ACT[act], bias\n        self.att_act = nn.LeakyReLU(negative_slope=0.2)\n        self.dropout = dropout\n        self._f_lin = []\n        self._offset, self._scale = [], []\n        self._attention = []\n        # mostly we have order = 1 for GAT\n        # \"+1\" since we do batch norm of order-0 and order-1 outputs separately\n        for o in range(self.order+1):\n            for i in range(self.mulhead):\n                self._f_lin.append(nn.Linear(dim_in, int(dim_out / self.mulhead), bias=True))\n                nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self._f_lin[-1].weight)\n                # _offset and _scale are for 'norm' type of batch norm\n                self._offset.append(nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(int(dim_out / self.mulhead))))\n                self._scale.append(nn.Parameter(torch.ones(int(dim_out / self.mulhead))))\n                self.num_param += dim_in * dim_out / self.mulhead + 2 * dim_out / self.mulhead\n                if o < self.order:\n                    self._attention.append(nn.Parameter(torch.ones(1, int(dim_out / self.mulhead * 2))))\n                    nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self._attention[-1])\n                    self.num_param += dim_out / self.mulhead * 2\n        self.mods = nn.ModuleList(self._f_lin)\n        self.f_dropout = nn.Dropout(p=self.dropout)\n        self.params = nn.ParameterList(self._offset + self._scale + self._attention)\n        self.f_lin = []\n        self.offset, self.scale = [], []\n        self.attention = []\n        # We need traverse order and mulhead the second time, just because we want to support\n        # higher order. Reason: if we have torch parameters in a python list, i.e.:\n        #       [nn.Parameter(), nn.Parameter(), ...]\n        # PyTorch cannot automically add these parameters into the learnable parameters.\n        for o in range(self.order+1):\n            self.f_lin.append([])\n            self.offset.append([])\n            self.scale.append([])\n            self.attention.append([])\n            for i in range(self.mulhead):\n                self.f_lin[-1].append(self.mods[o * self.mulhead + i])\n                if self.bias == 'norm':     # not used in 'norm-nn' mode\n                    self.offset[-1].append(self.params[o * self.mulhead + i])\n                    self.scale[-1].append(self.params[len(self._offset) + o * self.mulhead + i])\n                if o < self.order:      # excluding the order-0 part\n                    self.attention[-1].append(self.params[len(self._offset) * 2 + o * self.mulhead + i])\n        if self.bias == 'norm-nn':\n            final_dim_out = dim_out*((aggr=='concat')*(order+1) + (aggr=='mean'))\n            self.f_norm = nn.BatchNorm1d(final_dim_out, eps=1e-9, track_running_stats=True)\n        self.num_param = int(self.num_param)\n\n    def _spmm(self, adj_norm, _feat):\n        return torch.sparse.mm(adj_norm, _feat)\n\n    def _f_feat_trans(self, _feat, f_lin):\n        feat_out = []\n        for i in range(self.mulhead):\n            feat_out.append(self.act(f_lin[i](_feat)))\n        return feat_out\n\n    def _aggregate_attention(self, adj, feat_neigh, feat_self, attention):\n        attention_self = self.att_act(attention[:, : feat_self.shape[1]].mm(feat_self.t())).squeeze()\n        attention_neigh = self.att_act(attention[:, feat_neigh.shape[1] :].mm(feat_neigh.t())).squeeze()\n        attention_norm = (attention_self[adj._indices()[0]] + attention_neigh[adj._indices()[1]]) * adj._values()\n        att_adj = torch.sparse.FloatTensor(adj._indices(), attention_norm, torch.Size(adj.shape))\n        return self._spmm(att_adj, feat_neigh)\n\n    def forward(self, inputs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            inputs          tuple / list of two elements:\n                            1. feat_in: 2D matrix of node features input to the layer\n                            2. adj_norm: normalized subgraph adj. Normalization should\n                               consider both the node degree and aggregation normalization\n\n        Outputs:\n            feat_out        2D matrix of features for output nodes of the layer\n            adj_norm        normalized adj same as the input. We have to return it to\n                            support nn.Sequential called in models.py\n        \"\"\"\n        adj_norm, feat_in = inputs\n        feat_in = self.f_dropout(feat_in)\n        # generate A^i X\n        feat_partial = []\n        for o in range(self.order + 1):\n            feat_partial.append(self._f_feat_trans(feat_in, self.f_lin[o]))\n        for o in range(1,self.order + 1):\n            for s in range(o):\n                for i in range(self.mulhead):\n                    feat_partial[o][i] = self._aggregate_attention(\n                        adj_norm,\n                        feat_partial[o][i],\n                        feat_partial[o - s - 1][i],\n                        self.attention[o-1][i],\n                    )\n        if self.bias == 'norm':\n            # normalize per-order, per-head\n            for o in range(self.order + 1):\n                for i in range(self.mulhead):\n                    mean = feat_partial[o][i].mean(dim=1).unsqueeze(1)\n                    var = feat_partial[o][i].var(dim=1, unbiased=False).unsqueeze(1) + 1e-9\n                    feat_partial[o][i] = (feat_partial[o][i] - mean) \\\n                                * self.scale[o][i] * torch.rsqrt(var) + self.offset[o][i]\n\n        for o in range(self.order + 1):\n            feat_partial[o] = torch.cat(feat_partial[o], 1)\n        if self.aggr == 'mean':\n            feat_out = feat_partial[0]\n            for o in range(len(feat_partial) - 1):\n                feat_out += feat_partial[o + 1]\n        elif self.aggr == 'concat':\n            feat_out = torch.cat(feat_partial, 1)\n        else:\n            raise NotImplementedError\n        if self.bias == 'norm-nn':\n            feat_out = self.f_norm(feat_out)\n        return adj_norm, feat_out\n\n\nclass GatedAttentionAggregator(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"\n    Gated attentionn network (GaAN: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1803.07294.pdf).\n    The general idea of attention is similar to GAT. The main difference is that GaAN adds\n    a gated weight for each attention head. Therefore, we can selectively pick important\n    heads for better expressive power. Note that this layer is quite expensive to execute,\n    since the operations to compute attention are complicated. Therefore, we only support\n    order <= 1 (See HighOrderAggregator for definition of order).\n\n    Inputs:\n        dim_in      int, feature dimension for input nodes\n        dim_out     int, feature dimension for output nodes\n        dropout     float, dropout on weight matrices W_0 to W_k\n        act         str, activation function. See F_ACT at the top of this file\n        order       int, see definition in HighOrderAggregator\n        aggr        str, if 'mean' then [+] operation adds features of various hops\n                         if 'concat' then [+] concatenates features of various hops\n        bias        str, if 'bias' then apply a bias vector to features of each hop\n                         if 'norm' then perform batch-normalization on output features\n        mulhead     int, the number of heads for attention\n        dim_gate    int, output dimension of theta_m during gate value calculation\n\n    Outputs:\n        None\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        dim_in,\n        dim_out,\n        dropout=0.0,\n        act=\"relu\",\n        order=1,\n        aggr=\"mean\",\n        bias=\"norm\",\n        mulhead=1,\n        dim_gate=64,\n    ):\n        super(GatedAttentionAggregator, self).__init__()\n        self.num_param = 0      # TODO: update param count\n        self.multi_head = mulhead\n        assert self.multi_head > 0 and dim_out % self.multi_head == 0\n        self.order, self.aggr = order, aggr\n        self.act, self.bias = F_ACT[act], bias\n        self.att_act = nn.LeakyReLU(negative_slope=0.2)\n        self.dropout = dropout\n        self.dim_gate = dim_gate\n        self._f_lin = []\n        self._offset, self._scale = [], []\n        self._attention = []\n        for i in range(self.order + 1):\n            self._offset.append(nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(dim_out)))\n            self._scale.append(nn.Parameter(torch.ones(dim_out)))\n            for _j in range(self.multi_head):\n                self._f_lin.append(\n                    nn.Linear(dim_in, int(dim_out / self.multi_head), bias=True)\n                )\n                nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self._f_lin[-1].weight)\n                if i < self.order:\n                    self._attention.append(\n                        nn.Parameter(torch.ones(1, int(dim_out / self.multi_head * 2)))\n                    )\n                    nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self._attention[-1])\n        self._weight_gate = nn.Parameter(\n            torch.ones(dim_in * 2 + dim_gate, self.multi_head)\n        )\n        nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self._weight_gate)\n        self._weight_pool_gate = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(dim_in, dim_gate))\n        nn.init.xavier_uniform_(self._weight_pool_gate)\n        self.mods = nn.ModuleList(self._f_lin)\n        self.f_dropout = nn.Dropout(p=self.dropout)\n        self.params = nn.ParameterList(\n            self._offset\n            + self._scale\n            + self._attention\n            + [self._weight_gate, self._weight_pool_gate]\n        )\n        self.f_lin = []\n        self.offset, self.scale = [], []\n        self.attention = []\n        for i in range(self.order + 1):\n            self.f_lin.append([])\n            self.attention.append([])\n            self.offset.append(self.params[i])\n            self.scale.append(self.params[len(self._offset) + i])\n            for j in range(self.multi_head):\n                self.f_lin[-1].append(self.mods[i * self.multi_head + j])\n                if i < self.order:\n                    self.attention[-1].append(\n                        self.params[len(self._offset) * 2 + i * self.multi_head + j]\n                    )\n        self.weight_gate = self.params[-2]\n        self.weight_pool_gate = self.params[-1]\n\n    def _spmm(self, adj_norm, _feat):\n        return torch.sparse.mm(adj_norm, _feat)\n\n    def _f_feat_trans(self, _feat, f_lin):\n        feat_out = []\n        for i in range(self.multi_head):\n            feat_out.append(self.act(f_lin[i](_feat)))\n        return feat_out\n\n    def _aggregate_attention(self, adj, feat_neigh, feat_self, attention):\n        attention_self = self.att_act(\n            attention[:, : feat_self.shape[1]].mm(feat_self.t())\n        ).squeeze()\n        attention_neigh = self.att_act(\n            attention[:, feat_neigh.shape[1] :].mm(feat_neigh.t())\n        ).squeeze()\n        att_adj = torch.sparse.FloatTensor(\n            adj._indices(),\n            (attention_self[adj._indices()[0]] + attention_neigh[adj._indices()[1]])\n            * adj._values(),\n            torch.Size(adj.shape),\n        )\n        return self._spmm(att_adj, feat_neigh)\n\n    def _batch_norm(self, feat):\n        for i in range(self.order + 1):\n            mean = feat[i].mean(dim=1).unsqueeze(1)\n            var = feat[i].var(dim=1, unbiased=False).unsqueeze(1) + 1e-9\n            feat[i] = (feat[i] - mean) * self.scale[i] * torch.rsqrt(var) \\\n                    + self.offset[i]\n        return feat\n\n    def _compute_gate_value(self, adj, feat, adj_sp_csr):\n        \"\"\"\n        See equation (3) of the GaAN paper. Gate value is applied in front of each head.\n        Symbols such as zj follows the equations in the paper.\n        \"\"\"\n        zj = feat.mm(self.weight_pool_gate)\n        neigh_zj = []\n        # use loop instead since torch does not support sparse tensor slice\n        for i in range(adj.shape[0]):\n            if adj_sp_csr.indptr[i] < adj_sp_csr.indptr[i + 1]:\n                neigh_zj.append(\n                    torch.max(\n                        zj[\n                            adj_sp_csr.indices[\n                                adj_sp_csr.indptr[i] : adj_sp_csr.indptr[i + 1]\n                            ]\n                        ],\n                        0,\n                    )[0].unsqueeze(0)\n                )\n            else:\n                if zj.is_cuda:\n                    neigh_zj.append(torch.zeros(1, self.dim_gate).cuda())\n                else:\n                    neigh_zj.append(torch.zeros(1, self.dim_gate))\n        neigh_zj = torch.cat(neigh_zj, 0)\n        neigh_mean = self._spmm(adj, feat)\n        gate_feat = torch.cat([feat, neigh_zj, neigh_mean], 1)\n        return gate_feat.mm(self.weight_gate)\n\n    def forward(self, inputs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            inputs          tuple / list of two elements:\n                            1. adj_norm: normalized subgraph adj. Normalization should\n                               consider both the node degree and aggregation normalization\n                            2. feat_in: 2D matrix of node features input to the layer\n\n        Outputs:\n            feat_out        2D matrix of features for output nodes of the layer\n            adj_norm        normalized adj same as the input. We have to return it to\n                            support nn.Sequential called in models.py\n        \"\"\"\n        adj_norm, feat_in = inputs\n        feat_in = self.f_dropout(feat_in)\n        # compute gate value\n        adj_norm_cpu = adj_norm.cpu()\n        adj_norm_sp_csr = sp.coo_matrix(\n            (\n                adj_norm_cpu._values().numpy(),\n                (\n                    adj_norm_cpu._indices()[0].numpy(),\n                    adj_norm_cpu._indices()[1].numpy(),\n                ),\n            ),\n            shape=(adj_norm.shape[0], adj_norm.shape[0]),\n        ).tocsr()\n        gate_value = self._compute_gate_value(adj_norm, feat_in, adj_norm_sp_csr)\n        feat_partial = []\n        for i in range(self.order + 1):\n            feat_partial.append(self._f_feat_trans(feat_in, self.f_lin[i]))\n        for i in range(1, self.order + 1):\n            for j in range(i):\n                for k in range(self.multi_head):\n                    feat_partial[i][k] = self._aggregate_attention(\n                        adj_norm,\n                        feat_partial[i][k],\n                        feat_partial[i - j - 1][k],\n                        self.attention[i - 1][k],\n                    )\n                    feat_partial[i][k] *= gate_value[:, k].unsqueeze(1)\n        for i in range(self.order + 1):\n            feat_partial[i] = torch.cat(feat_partial[i], 1)\n        # if norm before concatenation, gate value vanishes\n        if self.bias == \"norm\":\n            feat_partial = self._batch_norm(feat_partial)\n        if self.aggr == \"mean\":\n            feat_out = feat_partial[0]\n            for i in range(len(feat_partial) - 1):\n                feat_out += feat_partial[i + 1]\n        elif self.aggr == \"concat\":\n            feat_out = torch.cat(feat_partial, 1)\n        else:\n            raise NotImplementedError\n        return adj_norm, feat_out\nfrom graphsaint.globals import *\nimport math\nfrom graphsaint.utils import *\nfrom graphsaint.graph_samplers import *\nfrom graphsaint.norm_aggr import *\nimport torch\nimport scipy.sparse as sp\nimport scipy\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport time\n\n\n\ndef _coo_scipy2torch(adj):\n    \"\"\"\n    convert a scipy sparse COO matrix to torch\n    \"\"\"\n    values = adj.data\n    indices = np.vstack((adj.row, adj.col))\n    i = torch.LongTensor(indices)\n    v = torch.FloatTensor(values)\n    return torch.sparse.FloatTensor(i,v, torch.Size(adj.shape))\n\n\n\nclass Minibatch:\n    \"\"\"\n    Provides minibatches for the trainer or evaluator. This class is responsible for\n    calling the proper graph sampler and estimating normalization coefficients.\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, adj_full_norm, adj_train, role, train_params, cpu_eval=False):\n        \"\"\"\n        Inputs:\n            adj_full_norm       scipy CSR, adj matrix for the full graph (row-normalized)\n            adj_train           scipy CSR, adj matrix for the traing graph. Since we are\n                                under transductive setting, for any edge in this adj,\n                                both end points must be training nodes.\n            role                dict, key 'tr' -> list of training node IDs;\n                                      key 'va' -> list of validation node IDs;\n                                      key 'te' -> list of test node IDs.\n            train_params        dict, additional parameters related to training. e.g.,\n                                how many subgraphs we want to get to estimate the norm\n                                coefficients.\n            cpu_eval            bool, whether or not we want to run full-batch evaluation\n                                on the CPU.\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.use_cuda = (args_global.gpu >= 0)\n        if cpu_eval:\n            self.use_cuda=False\n\n        self.node_train = np.array(role['tr'])\n        self.node_val = np.array(role['va'])\n        self.node_test = np.array(role['te'])\n\n        self.adj_full_norm = _coo_scipy2torch(adj_full_norm.tocoo())\n        self.adj_train = adj_train\n        # -----------------------\n        # sanity check (optional)\n        # -----------------------\n        #for role_set in [self.node_val, self.node_test]:\n        #    for v in role_set:\n        #        assert self.adj_train.indptr[v+1] == self.adj_train.indptr[v]\n        #_adj_train_T = sp.csr_matrix.tocsc(self.adj_train)\n        #assert np.abs(_adj_train_T.indices - self.adj_train.indices).sum() == 0\n        #assert np.abs(_adj_train_T.indptr - self.adj_train.indptr).sum() == 0\n        #_adj_full_T = sp.csr_matrix.tocsc(adj_full_norm)\n        #assert np.abs(_adj_full_T.indices - adj_full_norm.indices).sum() == 0\n        #assert np.abs(_adj_full_T.indptr - adj_full_norm.indptr).sum() == 0\n        #printf(\"SANITY CHECK PASSED\", style=\"yellow\")\n        if self.use_cuda:\n            # now i put everything on GPU. Ideally, full graph adj/feat\n            # should be optionally placed on CPU\n            self.adj_full_norm = self.adj_full_norm.cuda()\n\n        # below: book-keeping for mini-batch\n        self.node_subgraph = None\n        self.batch_num = -1\n\n        self.method_sample = None\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_indptr = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_indices = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_data = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_edge_index = []\n\n        self.norm_loss_train = np.zeros(self.adj_train.shape[0])\n        # norm_loss_test is used in full batch evaluation (without sampling).\n        # so neighbor features are simply averaged.\n        self.norm_loss_test = np.zeros(self.adj_full_norm.shape[0])\n        _denom = len(self.node_train) + len(self.node_val) +  len(self.node_test)\n        self.norm_loss_test[self.node_train] = 1. / _denom\n        self.norm_loss_test[self.node_val] = 1. / _denom\n        self.norm_loss_test[self.node_test] = 1. / _denom\n        self.norm_loss_test = torch.from_numpy(self.norm_loss_test.astype(np.float32))\n        if self.use_cuda:\n            self.norm_loss_test = self.norm_loss_test.cuda()\n        self.norm_aggr_train = np.zeros(self.adj_train.size)\n\n        self.sample_coverage = train_params['sample_coverage']\n        self.deg_train = np.array(self.adj_train.sum(1)).flatten()\n\n    def set_sampler(self, train_phases):\n        \"\"\"\n        Pick the proper graph sampler. Run the warm-up phase to estimate\n        loss / aggregation normalization coefficients.\n\n        Inputs:\n            train_phases       dict, config / params for the graph sampler\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_indptr = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_indices = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_data = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes = []\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_edge_index = []\n        self.method_sample = train_phases['sampler']\n        if self.method_sample == 'mrw':\n            if 'deg_clip' in train_phases:\n                _deg_clip = int(train_phases['deg_clip'])\n            else:\n                _deg_clip = 100000      # setting this to a large number so essentially there is no clipping in probability\n            self.size_subg_budget = train_phases['size_subgraph']\n            self.graph_sampler = mrw_sampling(\n                self.adj_train,\n                self.node_train,\n                self.size_subg_budget,\n                train_phases['size_frontier'],\n                _deg_clip,\n            )\n        elif self.method_sample == 'rw':\n            self.size_subg_budget = train_phases['num_root'] * train_phases['depth']\n            self.graph_sampler = rw_sampling(\n                self.adj_train,\n                self.node_train,\n                self.size_subg_budget,\n                int(train_phases['num_root']),\n                int(train_phases['depth']),\n            )\n        elif self.method_sample == 'edge':\n            self.size_subg_budget = train_phases['size_subg_edge'] * 2\n            self.graph_sampler = edge_sampling(\n                self.adj_train,\n                self.node_train,\n                train_phases['size_subg_edge'],\n            )\n        elif self.method_sample == 'node':\n            self.size_subg_budget = train_phases['size_subgraph']\n            self.graph_sampler = node_sampling(\n                self.adj_train,\n                self.node_train,\n                self.size_subg_budget,\n            )\n        elif self.method_sample == 'full_batch':\n            self.size_subg_budget = self.node_train.size\n            self.graph_sampler = full_batch_sampling(\n                self.adj_train,\n                self.node_train,\n                self.size_subg_budget,\n            )\n        elif self.method_sample == \"vanilla_node_python\":\n            self.size_subg_budget = train_phases[\"size_subgraph\"]\n            self.graph_sampler = NodeSamplingVanillaPython(\n                self.adj_train,\n                self.node_train,\n                self.size_subg_budget,\n            )\n        else:\n            raise NotImplementedError\n\n        self.norm_loss_train = np.zeros(self.adj_train.shape[0])\n        self.norm_aggr_train = np.zeros(self.adj_train.size).astype(np.float32)\n\n        # -------------------------------------------------------------\n        # BELOW: estimation of loss / aggregation normalization factors\n        # -------------------------------------------------------------\n        # For some special sampler, no need to estimate norm factors, we can calculate\n        # the node / edge probabilities directly.\n        # However, for integrity of the framework, we follow the same procedure\n        # for all samplers:\n        #   1. sample enough number of subgraphs\n        #   2. update the counter for each node / edge in the training graph\n        #   3. estimate norm factor alpha and lambda\n        tot_sampled_nodes = 0\n        while True:\n            self.par_graph_sample('train')\n            tot_sampled_nodes = sum([len(n) for n in self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes])\n            if tot_sampled_nodes > self.sample_coverage * self.node_train.size:\n                break\n        print()\n        num_subg = len(self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes)\n        for i in range(num_subg):\n            self.norm_aggr_train[self.subgraphs_remaining_edge_index[i]] += 1\n            self.norm_loss_train[self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes[i]] += 1\n        assert self.norm_loss_train[self.node_val].sum() + self.norm_loss_train[self.node_test].sum() == 0\n        for v in range(self.adj_train.shape[0]):\n            i_s = self.adj_train.indptr[v]\n            i_e = self.adj_train.indptr[v + 1]\n            val = np.clip(self.norm_loss_train[v] / self.norm_aggr_train[i_s : i_e], 0, 1e4)\n            val[np.isnan(val)] = 0.1\n            self.norm_aggr_train[i_s : i_e] = val\n        self.norm_loss_train[np.where(self.norm_loss_train==0)[0]] = 0.1\n        self.norm_loss_train[self.node_val] = 0\n        self.norm_loss_train[self.node_test] = 0\n        self.norm_loss_train[self.node_train] = num_subg / self.norm_loss_train[self.node_train] / self.node_train.size\n        self.norm_loss_train = torch.from_numpy(self.norm_loss_train.astype(np.float32))\n        if self.use_cuda:\n            self.norm_loss_train = self.norm_loss_train.cuda()\n\n    def par_graph_sample(self,phase):\n        \"\"\"\n        Perform graph sampling in parallel. A wrapper function for graph_samplers.py\n        \"\"\"\n        t0 = time.time()\n        _indptr, _indices, _data, _v, _edge_index = self.graph_sampler.par_sample(phase)\n        t1 = time.time()\n        print('sampling 200 subgraphs:   time = {:.3f} sec'.format(t1 - t0), end=\"\\r\")\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_indptr.extend(_indptr)\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_indices.extend(_indices)\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_data.extend(_data)\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes.extend(_v)\n        self.subgraphs_remaining_edge_index.extend(_edge_index)\n\n    def one_batch(self, mode='train'):\n        \"\"\"\n        Generate one minibatch for trainer. In the 'train' mode, one minibatch corresponds\n        to one subgraph of the training graph. In the 'val' or 'test' mode, one batch\n        corresponds to the full graph (i.e., full-batch rather than minibatch evaluation\n        for validation / test sets).\n\n        Inputs:\n            mode                str, can be 'train', 'val', 'test' or 'valtest'\n\n        Outputs:\n            node_subgraph       np array, IDs of the subgraph / full graph nodes\n            adj                 scipy CSR, adj matrix of the subgraph / full graph\n            norm_loss           np array, loss normalization coefficients. In 'val' or\n                                'test' modes, we don't need to normalize, and so the values\n                                in this array are all 1.\n        \"\"\"\n        if mode in ['val','test','valtest']:\n            self.node_subgraph = np.arange(self.adj_full_norm.shape[0])\n            adj = self.adj_full_norm\n        else:\n            assert mode == 'train'\n            if len(self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes) == 0:\n                self.par_graph_sample('train')\n                print()\n\n            self.node_subgraph = self.subgraphs_remaining_nodes.pop()\n            self.size_subgraph = len(self.node_subgraph)\n            adj = sp.csr_matrix(\n                (\n                    self.subgraphs_remaining_data.pop(),\n                    self.subgraphs_remaining_indices.pop(),\n                    self.subgraphs_remaining_indptr.pop()),\n                    shape=(self.size_subgraph,self.size_subgraph,\n                )\n            )\n            adj_edge_index = self.subgraphs_remaining_edge_index.pop()\n            #print(\"{} nodes, {} edges, {} degree\".format(self.node_subgraph.size,adj.size,adj.size/self.node_subgraph.size))\n            norm_aggr(adj.data, adj_edge_index, self.norm_aggr_train, num_proc=args_global.num_cpu_core)\n            # adj.data[:] = self.norm_aggr_train[adj_edge_index][:]      # this line is interchangable with the above line\n            adj = adj_norm(adj, deg=self.deg_train[self.node_subgraph])\n            adj = _coo_scipy2torch(adj.tocoo())\n            if self.use_cuda:\n                adj = adj.cuda()\n            self.batch_num += 1\n        norm_loss = self.norm_loss_test if mode in ['val','test', 'valtest'] else self.norm_loss_train\n        norm_loss = norm_loss[self.node_subgraph]\n        return self.node_subgraph, adj, norm_loss\n\n\n    def num_training_batches(self):\n        return math.ceil(self.node_train.shape[0] / float(self.size_subg_budget))\n\n    def shuffle(self):\n        self.node_train = np.random.permutation(self.node_train)\n        self.batch_num = -1\n\n    def end(self):\n        return (self.batch_num + 1) * self.size_subg_budget >= self.node_train.shape[0]\nimport torch\nfrom torch import nn\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\nimport numpy as np\nfrom graphsaint.utils import *\nimport graphsaint.pytorch_version.layers as layers\n\n\nclass GraphSAINT(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, num_classes, arch_gcn, train_params, feat_full, label_full, cpu_eval=False):\n        \"\"\"\n        Build the multi-layer GNN architecture.\n\n        Inputs:\n            num_classes         int, number of classes a node can belong to\n            arch_gcn            dict, config for each GNN layer\n            train_params        dict, training hyperparameters (e.g., learning rate)\n            feat_full           np array of shape N x f, where N is the total num of\n                                nodes and f is the dimension for input node feature\n            label_full          np array, for single-class classification, the shape\n                                is N x 1 and for multi-class classification, the\n                                shape is N x c (where c = num_classes)\n            cpu_eval            bool, if True, will put the model on CPU.\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        super(GraphSAINT,self).__init__()\n        self.use_cuda = (args_global.gpu >= 0)\n        if cpu_eval:\n            self.use_cuda=False\n        if \"attention\" in arch_gcn:\n            if \"gated_attention\" in arch_gcn:\n                if arch_gcn['gated_attention']:\n                    self.aggregator_cls = layers.GatedAttentionAggregator\n                    self.mulhead = int(arch_gcn['attention'])\n            else:\n                self.aggregator_cls = layers.AttentionAggregator\n                self.mulhead = int(arch_gcn['attention'])\n        else:\n            self.aggregator_cls = layers.HighOrderAggregator\n            self.mulhead = 1\n        self.num_layers = len(arch_gcn['arch'].split('-'))\n        self.weight_decay = train_params['weight_decay']\n        self.dropout = train_params['dropout']\n        self.lr = train_params['lr']\n        self.arch_gcn = arch_gcn\n        self.sigmoid_loss = (arch_gcn['loss'] == 'sigmoid')\n        self.feat_full = torch.from_numpy(feat_full.astype(np.float32))\n        self.label_full = torch.from_numpy(label_full.astype(np.float32))\n        if self.use_cuda:\n            self.feat_full = self.feat_full.cuda()\n            self.label_full = self.label_full.cuda()\n        if not self.sigmoid_loss:\n            self.label_full_cat = torch.from_numpy(label_full.argmax(axis=1).astype(np.int64))\n            if self.use_cuda:\n                self.label_full_cat = self.label_full_cat.cuda()\n        self.num_classes = num_classes\n        _dims, self.order_layer, self.act_layer, self.bias_layer, self.aggr_layer \\\n                        = parse_layer_yml(arch_gcn, self.feat_full.shape[1])\n        # get layer index for each conv layer, useful for jk net last layer aggregation\n        self.set_idx_conv()\n        self.set_dims(_dims)\n\n        self.loss = 0\n        self.opt_op = None\n\n        # build the model below\n        self.num_params = 0\n        self.aggregators, num_param = self.get_aggregators()\n        self.num_params += num_param\n        self.conv_layers = nn.Sequential(*self.aggregators)\n        self.classifier = layers.HighOrderAggregator(self.dims_feat[-1], self.num_classes,\\\n                            act='I', order=0, dropout=self.dropout, bias='bias')\n        self.num_params += self.classifier.num_param\n        self.optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(self.parameters(), lr=self.lr)\n\n    def set_dims(self, dims):\n        \"\"\"\n        Set the feature dimension / weight dimension for each GNN or MLP layer.\n        We will use the dimensions set here to initialize PyTorch layers.\n\n        Inputs:\n            dims        list, length of node feature for each hidden layer\n\n        Outputs:\n            None\n        \"\"\"\n        self.dims_feat = [dims[0]] + [\n            ((self.aggr_layer[l]=='concat') * self.order_layer[l] + 1) * dims[l+1]\n            for l in range(len(dims) - 1)\n        ]\n        self.dims_weight = [(self.dims_feat[l],dims[l+1]) for l in range(len(dims)-1)]\n\n    def set_idx_conv(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Set the index of GNN layers for the full neural net. For example, if\n        the full NN is having 1-0-1-0 arch (1-hop graph conv, followed by 0-hop\n        MLP, ...). Then the layer indices will be 0, 2.\n        \"\"\"\n        idx_conv = np.where(np.array(self.order_layer) >= 1)[0]\n        idx_conv = list(idx_conv[1:] - 1)\n        idx_conv.append(len(self.order_layer) - 1)\n        _o_arr = np.array(self.order_layer)[idx_conv]\n        if np.prod(np.ediff1d(_o_arr)) == 0:\n            self.idx_conv = idx_conv\n        else:\n            self.idx_conv = list(np.where(np.array(self.order_layer) == 1)[0])\n\n\n    def forward(self, node_subgraph, adj_subgraph):\n        feat_subg = self.feat_full[node_subgraph]\n        label_subg = self.label_full[node_subgraph]\n        label_subg_converted = label_subg if self.sigmoid_loss else self.label_full_cat[node_subgraph]\n        _, emb_subg = self.conv_layers((adj_subgraph, feat_subg))\n        emb_subg_norm = F.normalize(emb_subg, p=2, dim=1)\n        pred_subg = self.classifier((None, emb_subg_norm))[1]\n        return pred_subg, label_subg, label_subg_converted\n\n\n    def _loss(self, preds, labels, norm_loss):\n        \"\"\"\n        The predictor performs sigmoid (for multi-class) or softmax (for single-class)\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.sigmoid_loss:\n            norm_loss = norm_loss.unsqueeze(1)\n            return torch.nn.BCEWithLogitsLoss(weight=norm_loss,reduction='sum')(preds, labels)\n        else:\n            _ls = torch.nn.CrossEntropyLoss(reduction='none')(preds, labels)\n            return (norm_loss*_ls).sum()\n\n\n    def get_aggregators(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Return a list of aggregator instances. to be used in self.build()\n        \"\"\"\n        num_param = 0\n        aggregators = []\n        for l in range(self.num_layers):\n            aggr = self.aggregator_cls(\n                    *self.dims_weight[l],\n                    dropout=self.dropout,\n                    act=self.act_layer[l],\n                    order=self.order_layer[l],\n                    aggr=self.aggr_layer[l],\n                    bias=self.bias_layer[l],\n                    mulhead=self.mulhead,\n            )\n            num_param += aggr.num_param\n            aggregators.append(aggr)\n        return aggregators, num_param\n\n    def predict(self, preds):\n        return nn.Sigmoid()(preds) if self.sigmoid_loss else F.softmax(preds, dim=1)\n\n\n    def train_step(self, node_subgraph, adj_subgraph, norm_loss_subgraph):\n        \"\"\"\n        Forward and backward propagation\n        \"\"\"\n        self.train()\n        self.optimizer.zero_grad()\n        preds, labels, labels_converted = self(node_subgraph, adj_subgraph)\n        loss = self._loss(preds, labels_converted, norm_loss_subgraph) # labels.squeeze()?\n        loss.backward()\n        torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm(self.parameters(), 5)\n        self.optimizer.step()\n        return loss, self.predict(preds), labels\n\n    def eval_step(self, node_subgraph, adj_subgraph, norm_loss_subgraph):\n        \"\"\"\n        Forward propagation only\n        \"\"\"\n        self.eval()\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            preds,labels,labels_converted = self(node_subgraph, adj_subgraph)\n            loss = self._loss(preds,labels_converted,norm_loss_subgraph)\n        return loss, self.predict(preds), labels",

nodes\n        (e.g., those belonging to the val / test sets).\n    \"\"\"\n    loss,preds,labels = model.eval_step(*minibatch.one_batch(mode=mode))\n    if mode == 'val':\n        node_target = graphsaint.globals import *\n\n\ndef load_data(prefix, normalize=True):\n    \"\"\"\n    Load the various data files residing in the `prefix` directory.\n    Files to be loaded:\n        adj_full.npz        sparse matrix in CSR format, stored as scipy.sparse.csr_matrix\n                            The shape is N by N. Non-zeros in the matrix correspond to all\n                            the edges in the full graph. It doesn't matter if the two nodes\n                            connected by an edge are training, validation or test nodes.\n                            For unweighted graph, the non-zeros are all 1.\n        adj_train.npz       sparse matrix in CSR format, stored as a scipy.sparse.csr_matrix\n                            The shape is also N by N. However, non-zeros in the matrix only\n                            correspond to edges connecting two training nodes. The graph\n                            sampler only picks nodes/edges from this adj_train, not adj_full.\n                            Therefore, neither the attribute information nor the structural\n                            information are revealed during training. Also, note that only\n                            a x N rows and cols of adj_train contains non-zeros. For\n                            unweighted graph, the non-zeros are all 1.\n        role.json           a dict of three keys. Key 'tr' corresponds to the list of all\n                              'tr':     list of all training node indices\n                              'va':     list of all validation node indices\n                              'te':     list of all test node indices\n                            Note that in the raw data, nodes may have string-type ID. You\n                            need to re-assign numerical ID (0 to N-1) to the nodes, so that\n                            you can index into the matrices of adj, features and class labels.\n        class_map.json      a dict of length N. Each key is a node index, and each value is\n                            either a length C binary list (for multi-class classification)\n                            or an integer scalar (0 to C-1, for single-class classification).\n        feats.npz           a numpy array of shape N by F. Row i corresponds to the attribute\n                            vector of node i.\n\n    Inputs:\n        prefix              string, directory containing the above graph related files\n        normalize           bool, whether or not to normalize the node features\n\n    Outputs:\n        adj_full            scipy sparse CSR (shape N x N, |E| non-zeros), the adj matrix of\n                            the full graph, with N being total num of train + val + test nodes.\n        adj_train           scipy sparse CSR (shape N x N, |E'| non-zeros), the adj matrix of\n                            the training graph. While the shape is the same as adj_full, the\n                            rows/cols corresponding to val/test nodes in adj_train are all-zero.\n        feats               np array (shape N x f), the node feature matrix, with f being the\n                            length of each node feature vector.\n        class_map           dict, where key is the node ID and value is the classes this node\n                            belongs to.\n        role                dict, where keys are: 'tr' for train, 'va' for validation and 'te'\n                            for test nodes. The value is the list of IDs of nodes belonging to\n                            the train/val/test sets.\n    \"\"\"\n    adj_full = scipy.sparse.load_npz('./{}/adj_full.npz'.format(prefix)).astype(np.bool)\n    adj_train = scipy.sparse.load_npz('./{}/adj_train.npz'.format(prefix)).astype(np.bool)\n    role = json.load(open('./{}/role.json'.format(prefix)))\n    feats = np.load('./{}/feats.npy'.format(prefix))\n    class_map = json.load(open('./{}/class_map.json'.format(prefix)))\n    class_map = {int(k):v for k,v in class_map.items()}\n    assert len(class_map) == feats.shape[0]\n    # ---- normalize feats ----\n    train_nodes = np.array(list(set(adj_train.nonzero()[0])))\n    train_feats = feats[train_nodes]\n    scaler = StandardScaler()\n    scaler.fit(train_feats)\n    feats = scaler.transform(feats)\n    # -------------------------\n    return adj_full, adj_train, feats, class_map, role\n\n\ndef process_graph_data(adj_full, adj_train, feats, class_map, role):\n    \"\"\"\n    setup vertex property map for output classes, train/val/test masks, and feats\n    \"\"\"\n    num_vertices = adj_full.shape[0]\n    if isinstance(list(class_map.values())[0],list):\n        num_classes = len(list(class_map.values())[0])\n        class_arr = np.zeros((num_vertices, num_classes))\n        for k,v in class_map.items():\n            class_arr[k] = v\n    else:\n        num_classes = max(class_map.values()) - min(class_map.values()) + 1\n        class_arr = np.zeros((num_vertices, num_classes))\n        offset = min(class_map.values())\n        for k,v in class_map.items():\n            class_arr[k][v-offset] = 1\n    return adj_full, adj_train, feats, class_arr, role\n\n\ndef parse_layer_yml(arch_gcn,dim_input):\n    \"\"\"\n    Parse the *.yml config file to retrieve the GNN structure.\n    \"\"\"\n    num_layers = len(arch_gcn['arch'].split('-'))\n    # set default values, then update by arch_gcn\n    bias_layer = [arch_gcn['bias']]*num_layers\n    act_layer = [arch_gcn['act']]*num_layers\n    aggr_layer = [arch_gcn['aggr']]*num_layers\n    dims_layer = [arch_gcn['dim']]*num_layers\n    order_layer = [int(o) for o in arch_gcn['arch'].split('-')]\n    return [dim_input]+dims_layer,order_layer,act_layer,bias_layer,aggr_layer\n\n\ndef parse_n_prepare(flags):\n    with open(flags.train_config) as f_train_config:\n        train_config =
